2: #
3: #  sgirandompixel -- RUNG-3 WITNESS for dev_sgi_re.c horrible_getputpixel().
4: #
5: #  THE DEFECT.  horrible_getputpixel() extracts `tlb_mode = (mode >> 10) & 0x7' and switches on it.
6: #  Cases 0, 1, 2 and 4 are implemented.  The `default:' arm prints, has its exit(1) COMMENTED OUT,
7: #  and then executes `*color = random(); return;'.  So a failed GET does not decline -- it hands the
8: #  caller a random 32-bit value.  In draw_primitive() the failed source GET and the destination PUT
9: #  share one `color' variable in one loop iteration:
10: #
11: #      if (drawmode & DE_DRAWMODE_XFER_EN)
12: #              horrible_getputpixel(0, ..., src_x, src_y, &color, src_mode);   // may set random()
13: #      ...
14: #      if (draw)
15: #              horrible_getputpixel(1, ..., x, y, &color, dst_mode);           // writes it
16: #
17: #  AND THE UNHANDLED VALUE IS A DOCUMENTED MODE.  src/include/thirdparty/crmfbreg.h defines five:
18: #    DE_MODE_TLB_A 0x0000 -> 0 handled     DE_MODE_LIN_A 0x1000 -> 4 handled
19: #    DE_MODE_TLB_B 0x0400 -> 1 handled     DE_MODE_LIN_B 0x1400 -> 5 *** default -> random() ***
20: #    DE_MODE_TLB_C 0x0800 -> 2 handled
21: #
22: #  CONTRACT (vacuity-floor clause (e)) -- what this witness catches and what it does NOT:
23: #    CATCHES   the default arm substituting a value instead of declining: `copy lin_b' reds if the
24: #              destination receives anything other than a value distinct from BOTH the source and
25: #              the pre-painted destination.  Restoring `exit(1)' there kills the machine and reds
26: #              every row.  Making the arm leave *color untouched turns `copy lin_b' green-by-accident
27: #              ONLY if the pre-paint is removed -- which is why the destination is PRE-PAINTED with a
28: #              known value and the row rejects it: "left alone" and "wrote random" are then distinct.
29: #    DOES NOT  establish that any real driver selects LIN_B.  It appears only in crmfbreg.h and the

31: #              there is no O2/sgimips image in the tree to boot.  **Documented and storable is NOT
32: #              driver-selected**, and this probe claims only the former (grok, the witness panel).
33: #
34: #  DESIGN CREDIT AND THE FALSE NEGATIVE IT AVOIDS (grok, panel_20260906_224008).  The obvious probe --
35: #  set the source mode to LIN_B, draw, read the destination -- FINDS NOTHING.  At reset the
36: #  destination mode is TLB_A and re_tlb_a[] has bit 31 clear, so the PUT returns WITHOUT WRITING and
37: #  the destination is unchanged: a false negative that looks like a clean run.  Using LIN_A as the
38: #  DESTINATION closes it, because case 4 forces the valid bit:

40: #  so re_linear_a[0] = 0x10 puts the tile at physical 0x10000, and the byte offset is x itself.
41: #
42: #  THE ORACLE IS CERTAIN, NOT PROBABILISTIC.  glibc random() is 31-bit, so the value written has bit
43: #  31 CLEAR and can never equal a source of 0xAABBCCDD.  Inequality is guaranteed, not 2^-32.

95: def row(name, got, want, note="", negate=False):
96:     ok = (got != want) if negate else (got == want)
97:     rows.append((name, ok))

102: def session(cmds, settle=1.5, spin=1.6):
103:     pid, fd = pty.fork()
104:     if pid == 0:
105:         os.execv(BIN, [BIN, "-V", "-x", "-E", "sgi", "-e", "ip32", "-M", "64",
106:                        "0x80001000:" + DUMMY])

108:     buf = ""
109:     def rd(t):
110:         nonlocal buf
111:         end = time.time() + t
112:         while time.time() < end:
113:             r, _, _ = select.select([fd], [], [], 0.1)
114:             if not r:
115:                 continue
116:             try:
117:                 d = os.read(fd, 65536)
118:                 if not d:
119:                     return False
120:                 buf += d.decode("latin-1", "replace")
121:             except OSError:
122:                 return False
123:         return True

128:         except OSError:

138:     try:

144: def printed(t, reg):
145:     #  MASK TO 32 BITS.  `lw' sign-extends into a 64-bit register, so a value with bit 31 set
146:     #  reads back as 0xffffffffaabbccdd and a row comparing raw strings fails on FORMATTING
147:     #  rather than on behaviour -- measured on this probe's first run.  The shipped
148:     #  sgi_re_width_probe.py carries the same normalisation for the same reason.
149:     m = re.search(r"print\s+%s\s*\n\s*(0x[0-9a-f]+)" % reg, t)
150:     return "absent" if not m else "0x%x" % (int(m.group(1), 16) & 0xffffffff)

152: class Addr(object):
153:     def __init__(self, base): self.a = base
154:     def take(self, n):
155:         got = self.a; self.a += 4 * (n + 2) + 0x20; return got
156: 
157: def run_ops(ops, at):
158:     put = ["put w 0x%x, 0x%08x" % (at + 4 * i, op) for i, op in enumerate(ops)]
159:     put += ["put w 0x%x, 0x%08x" % (at + 4 * len(ops), OP_B_SELF),
160:             "put w 0x%x, 0x%08x" % (at + 4 * len(ops) + 4, OP_NOP)]
161:     return put + ["pc=0x%x" % at, "step %d" % len(ops)]
162: 
163: def store(A, addr, val, op=OP_SW_T0_A3):
164:     return ["a3=0x%x" % addr, "t0=0x%x" % val] + run_ops([op], A.take(1))
165: 
166: SENTINEL = 0xdeadbeef
167: def load(A, addr):
168:     return ["at=0x%x" % addr, "v0=0x%x" % SENTINEL] + run_ops([OP_LW_V0_AT], A.take(1))
169: 
170: def setup(A, src_mode):
171:     """Program the engine, paint source and destination, then fire one rectangle.
172:        Every store is a REAL GUEST INSTRUCTION through memory_rw -- no device_add,
173:        no debugger `put' to a device register."""
174:     #  *** THE 64-BIT STORE WRITES THE HIGH WORD TO [n] AND THE LOW WORD TO [n+1]. ***  Measured:
175:     #  `sd' of 0x10 gave `re_linear_a[0] = 0, [1] = 0x10', so the tile pointer was 0 and every pixel
176:     #  went to PHYSICAL ADDRESS 0 -- the destination stayed at its pre-paint and the ACCEPT control
177:     #  failed, which is precisely the "do not leave it 0: that writes phys 0" trap grok named.  The
178:     #  value below puts 0x10 in the HIGH word so re_linear_a[0] = 0x10.
179:     c  = store(A, RE_LINEAR_A, 0x0000001000000000, op=OP_SD_T0_A3)  # sd: a 4-byte sw is DECLINED here
180:     c += store(A, DST_KSEG1, DST_PAINT)
181:     c += store(A, SRC_KSEG1, SRC_VAL)
182:     c += store(A, DE_MODE_DST,   MODE_LIN_A)
183:     c += store(A, DE_MODE_SRC,   src_mode)
184:     c += store(A, DE_DRAWMODE,   DRAWMODE)
185:     c += store(A, DE_XFER_ADDR,  SRC_OFS)              # linear: src_x = addr_src, src_y = 0
186:     c += store(A, DE_XFER_STEPX, 4)                    # must equal src bufdepth or the XFER arm warns
187:     c += store(A, DE_FG,         FG_VAL)               # what a declined GET leaves in `color'
188:     c += store(A, DE_XVERTEX_0,  0x00000000)           # (x1,y1) = (0,0)
189:     #  *** (x2,y2) = (0,3): ONE COLUMN, FOUR ROWS.  The height is load-bearing and the width must
190:     #  NOT be used instead. ***  `lin_b diag once' pins that the announce is LATCHED, and its channel
191:     #  is the announcement COUNT -- which cannot vary at all if the rectangle is one pixel, because an
192:     #  unlatched announce then also fires exactly once.  Measured: with (0,0)-(0,0) the M_NOLATCH
193:     #  mutant scored 8/8, a clean escape, and the row was VACUOUS BY CONSTRUCTION.
194:     #  It is the HEIGHT and not the width because LIN_A addressing is `ofs = x & 4095' -- y is
195:     #  ignored -- so four ROWS write the same byte four times (the value oracle survives) while four
196:     #  COLUMNS would write overlapping bytes at ofs 0..3 and destroy it.
197:     c += store(A, DE_XVERTEX_1,  0x00000003)           # (x2,y2) = (0,3): 4 pixels, all at ofs 0
198:     c += store(A, DE_PRIM_START, PRIM_RECT)            # PRIMITIVE|START fires the draw
199:     return c

234: #  `*color = random()' assignment is deleted, because the caller's `uint32_t color = fg' then stands.
235: #  It also discriminates every REJECTED disposition: zeroing leaves 0, suppressing the PUT leaves the
236: #  pre-paint, implementing LIN_B leaves the source.  Only "leave *color alone" leaves FG.
237: row("lin_b is fg", got_b, "0x%x" % FG_VAL,
238:     "FIX: a declined GET leaves *color, so the PUT writes FG")

245: #  the message text, and this helper is PER-PIXEL, so an unlatched announce is a per-pixel flood.  This
246: #  row is the discriminator against a SILENT decline (member 2), which would emit nothing at all.

codex
There is a concrete problem with the MTE premise: `depth` is calculated as 8, 16, 32, or 64, but the RGBA construction is guarded by `depth == 4`. That branch cannot execute. I also verified that the pixel buffer is `uint8_t` and the packing expressions shift promoted signed integers; changing only the byte indices would leave that separate arithmetic issue.

287:     #  twice and the run looked like the best table in the file's history.  The
288:     #  gate would have caught it -- its per-row check wants exactly 1 -- but only
289:     #  after a 15-minute run and only as an unexplained got=2.  One line here
290:     #  fails at the point of construction instead. ***

The distinct bytes in `aa bb cc dd` should expose in-bounds index substitutions and valid shifts changed by eight. There are still coverage gaps: changing `+` to `|` is equivalent with unsigned, disjoint byte fields, and a padding-mask error can pass if it preserves only bits that happen to be zero in `dd`. I’ll distinguish those cases from the caller and mode paths the inlined measurement does not cover.

codex
VERDICT: GET is the best-supported repair site for this emulator’s RGB layout; hardware fidelity and real-driver usage remain unproved. The cited MTE RGBA branch is unreachable.

I read the complete helper and its callers in the permitted tree. S1 and S3 match. No builds, probes, or gates were run.

**Q1. There is a coherent argument that PUT is wrong:** hardware RGB might mean memory bytes `[X,R,G,B]`, while the helper’s internal color representation is `0xRRGGBBAA`. Under that contract, the existing GET correctly discards byte 0, and PUT should store `[0,R,G,B]`. RGBA could independently use `[R,G,B,A]`.

Consequently, **S4 does not distinguish those hardware contracts**: interpreting raw `aa bb cc dd` as `[X,R,G,B]` legitimately produces `0xbbccdd00`. The explicit zero in PUT establishes the implementation’s chosen padding position, not the hardware’s.

What tips the in-tree evidence toward GET is the independent framebuffer consumer described below. Ordinary endianness does not reconcile the two arms: they explicitly assemble bytes, and the RAM path copies those bytes without rearrangement. [Pixel helper](C:/DocumentNoSnc/CC/GXEMUL/GXEMUL-SEC/src/devices/dev_sgi_re.c:184), [RAM copying](C:/DocumentNoSnc/CC/GXEMUL/GXEMUL-SEC/src/cpus/memory_rw.c:778).

S2 also needs a precise contract:

- CI preserves the low eight bits, not an arbitrary 32-bit input.
- Corrected RGB should preserve `color & 0xffffff00`.
- RGBA/ABGR byte-layout round trips assume four-byte transfers and successful accesses; color mode does not enforce buffer depth.
- S4 is a raw-memory-to-RGB-GET test. An actual RGB PUT of `0xaabbccdd` writes `aa bb cc 00`, whose current GET predicts `0xbbcc0000`.

**Q2. The framebuffer consumer supports `[R,G,B,X]`; I do not know the hardware contract.**

In `dev_sgi_gbe_tick()`, the four-byte path constructs a word from memory bytes in descending significance. `get_rgb()` then handles `CRMFB_MODE_TYP_RGB8` by extracting bits 31–24, 23–16, and 15–8. Together, these select memory bytes **0, 1, and 2**, agreeing with RGB PUT and the proposed GET correction. [Framebuffer byte assembly](C:/DocumentNoSnc/CC/GXEMUL/GXEMUL-SEC/src/devices/dev_sgi_gbe.c:281), [RGB extraction](C:/DocumentNoSnc/CC/GXEMUL/GXEMUL-SEC/src/devices/dev_sgi_gbe.c:128).

The RGB8 case says “Used by NetBSD’s X11 server.” That is useful evidence about the emulator’s display convention, but **GBE’s `CRMFB_MODE_TYP_RGB8` is a separate register format from RE’s `DE_MODE_TYPE_RGB`**. It does not prove that driver selects the RE format.

The imported NetBSD header names the RE modes without specifying their byte layout. Both device implementations describe themselves as guesswork. I found no authoritative hardware layout specification in the permitted sources. [Mode definitions](C:/DocumentNoSnc/CC/GXEMUL/GXEMUL-SEC/src/include/thirdparty/crmfbreg.h:319), [RE provenance](C:/DocumentNoSnc/CC/GXEMUL/GXEMUL-SEC/src/devices/dev_sgi_re.c:28).

**Q3. Several consumers change; I found no compensating channel shift.** Specifically, I checked:

| Consumer | Effect of correcting RGB GET |
|---|---|
| Rectangle source transfer | RGB source values change before destination PUT. Later RGB copies also use the corrected interpretation. |
| Line destination read for raster operations | RGB `oldcolor` changes, affecting implemented XOR and the operation labeled `COPY_INVERTED`. |
| Rectangle destination read for raster operations | Same effect, including when the source is RGBA. |
| GBE scanout | Existing memory is interpreted identically; pixels produced by affected copies or raster operations display differently. |
| MTE helper calls | Currently cannot select RGB; see Q4. |
| Diagnostics and framebuffer storage | RE diagnostics print registers and modes, not a compensating pixel transformation. Optional framebuffer debug dumps reflect delivered bytes. |

The destination-read sites are particularly important: coverage confined to RGB **source** copies misses them. [Line consumers](C:/DocumentNoSnc/CC/GXEMUL/GXEMUL-SEC/src/devices/dev_sgi_re.c:790), [Rectangle consumers](C:/DocumentNoSnc/CC/GXEMUL/GXEMUL-SEC/src/devices/dev_sgi_re.c:851), [Framebuffer diagnostics](C:/DocumentNoSnc/CC/GXEMUL/GXEMUL-SEC/src/devices/dev_fb.c:703), [Framebuffer storage](C:/DocumentNoSnc/CC/GXEMUL/GXEMUL-SEC/src/devices/dev_fb.c:787).

This establishes the dependency paths, not that an external guest has never compensated for the current behavior.

**Q4. RGB is guest-selectable through the ordinary device interface; actual driver selection remains unestablished.**

IP32 installs the rendering engine. The DE handler stores guest-written source and destination modes without filtering out RGB; `draw_primitive()` loads those registers and passes them to the helper. The helper has no probe-specific entry requirement. [IP32 initialization](C:/DocumentNoSnc/CC/GXEMUL/GXEMUL-SEC/src/machines/machine_sgi.c:499), [Register storage](C:/DocumentNoSnc/CC/GXEMUL/GXEMUL-SEC/src/devices/dev_sgi_re.c:978), [Mode consumption](C:/DocumentNoSnc/CC/GXEMUL/GXEMUL-SEC/src/devices/dev_sgi_re.c:675).

The source comment that OpenBSD draws characters through XFER establishes use of that mechanism, **not RGB mode**. Searches found no in-tree driver selecting `DE_MODE_TYPE_RGB`. I would record “guest-visible rendering correctness defect; real-driver incidence unestablished.”

The MTE premise needs correction:

- `int depth = 8 << (...)` yields **8, 16, 32, or 64**.
- The RGBA insertion is guarded by **`if (depth == 4)`**.
- That condition cannot hold. The constructed modes therefore retain color type zero, **CI**, rather than selecting RGBA.

This is a separate defect, not evidence for RGB reachability. [Depth declaration](C:/DocumentNoSnc/CC/GXEMUL/GXEMUL-SEC/src/devices/dev_sgi_re.c:1288), [Mode construction and impossible condition](C:/DocumentNoSnc/CC/GXEMUL/GXEMUL-SEC/src/devices/dev_sgi_re.c:1342).

**Q5. The brief supplies one RGB data case and three controls, not three specified RGB test vectors.** The controls alone cannot detect mutations confined to RGB GET. I found no RGB detector in the permitted regression Python files, so the following is analytical coverage, not measured mutation scores.

Assuming the repaired data row checks exactly `0xaabbcc00`, and packing uses unsigned arithmetic:

| Small mutation | Would that data row detect it? |
|---|---|
| Red index `0 → 1` | Yes: `0xbbbbcc00`. |
| Blue index `2 → 3` | Yes: `0xaabbdd00`. |
| Blue shift `8 → 0` | Yes: `0xaabb00cc`. Other valid shifts changed by eight also differ. |
| Restore all old indices | Yes: `0xbbccdd00`. |
| Remove RGB’s `break`, falling into RGBA | Yes: `0xaabbccdd`. A test using only RGB-PUT-generated zero padding would miss it. |
| Change `+` to bitwise OR | **Equivalent** for unsigned, disjoint byte fields. No detector should be required to kill it. |
| Change assignment `=` to `\|=` | Can escape if incoming `*color` is zero or contains only bits already present in the result. Use a distinct foreground value. |
| Remove a cast that makes a shift unsigned | Ordinary value checks may pass despite restoring undefined behavior. |

A particularly small survivor exists **if the chosen implementation uses word assembly followed by a mask**: changing `0xffffff00` to `0xffffff02` preserves padding bit 1. Your padding byte `dd` has that bit clear, so the faulty mask passes. Changing the mask to `0xfffffe00` also passes this sample because `cc` already has its least significant bit clear.

The detector should therefore cover:

- Independent RGB PUT byte verification and independently seeded RGB GET, preventing mutually compensating mistakes.
- Exact RGB-to-RGBA output, including the zero low byte.
- Isolated red, green, and blue values, with both zero and set bits; retain the distinct mixed-byte case.
- Identical RGB bytes with padding `00` and `ff`.
- A distinct foreground and destination prepaint.
- An RGB destination raster-operation read, plus CI/RGBA/ABGR controls. A linear-only test does not establish tiled-path coverage.

**Two additional issues matter to the design.** First, `buf` really is `uint8_t[8]`, but its elements promote to signed `int` before shifting. On a 32-bit-`int` C implementation, `buf[0] << 24` is undefined when the byte is at least `0x80`; assigning afterward to `uint32_t` does not repair that. An index-only edit retains this issue for your `aa` sample. The tree already has unsigned byte assembly helpers illustrating the appropriate arithmetic. [Unsigned assembly helpers](C:/DocumentNoSnc/CC/GXEMUL/GXEMUL-SEC/src/include/misc.h:196).

Second, buffer depth and color type are independent, while initialization and decoding assume particular widths. The impossible MTE condition consequently routes multibyte transfers through CI, whose PUT initializes only one byte. This warrants a separate review and prevents treating S2’s round-trip table as universal across depth settings. [Buffer initialization and transfer](C:/DocumentNoSnc/CC/GXEMUL/GXEMUL-SEC/src/devices/dev_sgi_re.c:284).
tokens used
122,390
VERDICT: GET is the best-supported repair site for this emulator’s RGB layout; hardware fidelity and real-driver usage remain unproved. The cited MTE RGBA branch is unreachable.

I read the complete helper and its callers in the permitted tree. S1 and S3 match. No builds, probes, or gates were run.

**Q1. There is a coherent argument that PUT is wrong:** hardware RGB might mean memory bytes `[X,R,G,B]`, while the helper’s internal color representation is `0xRRGGBBAA`. Under that contract, the existing GET correctly discards byte 0, and PUT should store `[0,R,G,B]`. RGBA could independently use `[R,G,B,A]`.

Consequently, **S4 does not distinguish those hardware contracts**: interpreting raw `aa bb cc dd` as `[X,R,G,B]` legitimately produces `0xbbccdd00`. The explicit zero in PUT establishes the implementation’s chosen padding position, not the hardware’s.

What tips the in-tree evidence toward GET is the independent framebuffer consumer described below. Ordinary endianness does not reconcile the two arms: they explicitly assemble bytes, and the RAM path copies those bytes without rearrangement. [Pixel helper](C:/DocumentNoSnc/CC/GXEMUL/GXEMUL-SEC/src/devices/dev_sgi_re.c:184), [RAM copying](C:/DocumentNoSnc/CC/GXEMUL/GXEMUL-SEC/src/cpus/memory_rw.c:778).

S2 also needs a precise contract:

- CI preserves the low eight bits, not an arbitrary 32-bit input.
- Corrected RGB should preserve `color & 0xffffff00`.
- RGBA/ABGR byte-layout round trips assume four-byte transfers and successful accesses; color mode does not enforce buffer depth.
- S4 is a raw-memory-to-RGB-GET test. An actual RGB PUT of `0xaabbccdd` writes `aa bb cc 00`, whose current GET predicts `0xbbcc0000`.

**Q2. The framebuffer consumer supports `[R,G,B,X]`; I do not know the hardware contract.**

In `dev_sgi_gbe_tick()`, the four-byte path constructs a word from memory bytes in descending significance. `get_rgb()` then handles `CRMFB_MODE_TYP_RGB8` by extracting bits 31–24, 23–16, and 15–8. Together, these select memory bytes **0, 1, and 2**, agreeing with RGB PUT and the proposed GET correction. [Framebuffer byte assembly](C:/DocumentNoSnc/CC/GXEMUL/GXEMUL-SEC/src/devices/dev_sgi_gbe.c:281), [RGB extraction](C:/DocumentNoSnc/CC/GXEMUL/GXEMUL-SEC/src/devices/dev_sgi_gbe.c:128).

The RGB8 case says “Used by NetBSD’s X11 server.” That is useful evidence about the emulator’s display convention, but **GBE’s `CRMFB_MODE_TYP_RGB8` is a separate register format from RE’s `DE_MODE_TYPE_RGB`**. It does not prove that driver selects the RE format.

The imported NetBSD header names the RE modes without specifying their byte layout. Both device implementations describe themselves as guesswork. I found no authoritative hardware layout specification in the permitted sources. [Mode definitions](C:/DocumentNoSnc/CC/GXEMUL/GXEMUL-SEC/src/include/thirdparty/crmfbreg.h:319), [RE provenance](C:/DocumentNoSnc/CC/GXEMUL/GXEMUL-SEC/src/devices/dev_sgi_re.c:28).

**Q3. Several consumers change; I found no compensating channel shift.** Specifically, I checked:

| Consumer | Effect of correcting RGB GET |
|---|---|
| Rectangle source transfer | RGB source values change before destination PUT. Later RGB copies also use the corrected interpretation. |
| Line destination read for raster operations | RGB `oldcolor` changes, affecting implemented XOR and the operation labeled `COPY_INVERTED`. |
| Rectangle destination read for raster operations | Same effect, including when the source is RGBA. |
| GBE scanout | Existing memory is interpreted identically; pixels produced by affected copies or raster operations display differently. |
| MTE helper calls | Currently cannot select RGB; see Q4. |
| Diagnostics and framebuffer storage | RE diagnostics print registers and modes, not a compensating pixel transformation. Optional framebuffer debug dumps reflect delivered bytes. |

The destination-read sites are particularly important: coverage confined to RGB **source** copies misses them. [Line consumers](C:/DocumentNoSnc/CC/GXEMUL/GXEMUL-SEC/src/devices/dev_sgi_re.c:790), [Rectangle consumers](C:/DocumentNoSnc/CC/GXEMUL/GXEMUL-SEC/src/devices/dev_sgi_re.c:851), [Framebuffer diagnostics](C:/DocumentNoSnc/CC/GXEMUL/GXEMUL-SEC/src/devices/dev_fb.c:703), [Framebuffer storage](C:/DocumentNoSnc/CC/GXEMUL/GXEMUL-SEC/src/devices/dev_fb.c:787).

This establishes the dependency paths, not that an external guest has never compensated for the current behavior.

**Q4. RGB is guest-selectable through the ordinary device interface; actual driver selection remains unestablished.**

IP32 installs the rendering engine. The DE handler stores guest-written source and destination modes without filtering out RGB; `draw_primitive()` loads those registers and passes them to the helper. The helper has no probe-specific entry requirement. [IP32 initialization](C:/DocumentNoSnc/CC/GXEMUL/GXEMUL-SEC/src/machines/machine_sgi.c:499), [Register storage](C:/DocumentNoSnc/CC/GXEMUL/GXEMUL-SEC/src/devices/dev_sgi_re.c:978), [Mode consumption](C:/DocumentNoSnc/CC/GXEMUL/GXEMUL-SEC/src/devices/dev_sgi_re.c:675).

The source comment that OpenBSD draws characters through XFER establishes use of that mechanism, **not RGB mode**. Searches found no in-tree driver selecting `DE_MODE_TYPE_RGB`. I would record “guest-visible rendering correctness defect; real-driver incidence unestablished.”

The MTE premise needs correction:

- `int depth = 8 << (...)` yields **8, 16, 32, or 64**.
- The RGBA insertion is guarded by **`if (depth == 4)`**.
- That condition cannot hold. The constructed modes therefore retain color type zero, **CI**, rather than selecting RGBA.

This is a separate defect, not evidence for RGB reachability. [Depth declaration](C:/DocumentNoSnc/CC/GXEMUL/GXEMUL-SEC/src/devices/dev_sgi_re.c:1288), [Mode construction and impossible condition](C:/DocumentNoSnc/CC/GXEMUL/GXEMUL-SEC/src/devices/dev_sgi_re.c:1342).

**Q5. The brief supplies one RGB data case and three controls, not three specified RGB test vectors.** The controls alone cannot detect mutations confined to RGB GET. I found no RGB detector in the permitted regression Python files, so the following is analytical coverage, not measured mutation scores.

Assuming the repaired data row checks exactly `0xaabbcc00`, and packing uses unsigned arithmetic:

| Small mutation | Would that data row detect it? |
|---|---|
| Red index `0 → 1` | Yes: `0xbbbbcc00`. |
| Blue index `2 → 3` | Yes: `0xaabbdd00`. |
| Blue shift `8 → 0` | Yes: `0xaabb00cc`. Other valid shifts changed by eight also differ. |
| Restore all old indices | Yes: `0xbbccdd00`. |
| Remove RGB’s `break`, falling into RGBA | Yes: `0xaabbccdd`. A test using only RGB-PUT-generated zero padding would miss it. |
| Change `+` to bitwise OR | **Equivalent** for unsigned, disjoint byte fields. No detector should be required to kill it. |
| Change assignment `=` to `\|=` | Can escape if incoming `*color` is zero or contains only bits already present in the result. Use a distinct foreground value. |
| Remove a cast that makes a shift unsigned | Ordinary value checks may pass despite restoring undefined behavior. |

A particularly small survivor exists **if the chosen implementation uses word assembly followed by a mask**: changing `0xffffff00` to `0xffffff02` preserves padding bit 1. Your padding byte `dd` has that bit clear, so the faulty mask passes. Changing the mask to `0xfffffe00` also passes this sample because `cc` already has its least significant bit clear.

The detector should therefore cover:

- Independent RGB PUT byte verification and independently seeded RGB GET, preventing mutually compensating mistakes.
- Exact RGB-to-RGBA output, including the zero low byte.
- Isolated red, green, and blue values, with both zero and set bits; retain the distinct mixed-byte case.
- Identical RGB bytes with padding `00` and `ff`.
- A distinct foreground and destination prepaint.
- An RGB destination raster-operation read, plus CI/RGBA/ABGR controls. A linear-only test does not establish tiled-path coverage.
